- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Large Language Models Trigger a Paradigm Shift in Travel Behavior Modeling? Experiences with Modeling Travel Satisfaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23262v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Xu, Donggen Wang
- **Abstract**: As a specific domain of subjective well-being, travel satisfaction has attracted much research attention recently. Previous studies primarily use statistical models and, more recently, machine learning models to explore the determinants of travel satisfaction. Both approaches require data from sufficient sample sizes and correct prior statistical assumptions. The emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) offers a new modeling approach that can overcome the shortcomings of the existing methods. Pre-trained on extensive datasets, LLMs have strong capabilities in contextual understanding and generalization, significantly reducing their dependence on large quantities of task-specific data and stringent statistical assumptions. The primary challenge in applying LLMs lies in addressing the behavioral misalignment between LLMs and human behavior. Using data on travel satisfaction from a household survey in shanghai, this study identifies the existence and source of misalignment and develop methods to address the misalignment issue. We find that the zero-shot LLM exhibits behavioral misalignment, resulting in relatively low prediction accuracy. However, few-shot learning, even with a limited number of samples, allows the model to outperform baseline models in MSE and MAPE metrics. This misalignment can be attributed to the gap between the general knowledge embedded in LLMs and the specific, unique characteristics of the dataset. On these bases, we propose an LLM-based modeling approach that can be applied to model travel behavior using samples of small sizes. This study highlights the potential of LLMs for modeling not only travel satisfaction but also broader aspects of travel behavior.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Can Large Language Models Trigger a Paradigm Shift in Travel Behavior Modeling? Experiences with Modeling Travel Satisfaction" explores the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) to travel behavior modeling, specifically focusing on travel satisfaction. The authors investigate the potential of LLMs to overcome limitations of traditional statistical and machine learning approaches, which typically require large datasets and may make strong prior assumptions. They address the issue of "behavioral misalignment" between LLMs and human behavior by using few-shot learning techniques to align the LLM's predictions with observed travel satisfaction data from a household survey in Shanghai. The results suggest that LLMs, especially with few-shot learning, can achieve comparable or even better performance than traditional methods with significantly smaller sample sizes, potentially leading to a paradigm shift in how travel behavior data is collected and modeled. The paper analyzes the reasoning behind LLM predictions and identifies the sources of misalignment, finding that it stems from the discordance between the LLM's general knowledge and the unique characteristics of the dataset. They also address the challenges in applying the framework in realistic contexts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty in Application:** Applying LLMs to travel satisfaction modeling is a relatively new area, and the paper provides a valuable initial exploration.
    *   **Addressing Misalignment:** The paper directly tackles the crucial issue of behavioral misalignment, which is essential for ensuring the reliability of LLM-based behavioral models. The few-shot learning approach is a relevant strategy for mitigating this issue.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The study uses real-world travel satisfaction data to empirically test the LLM-based approach, providing practical insights into its performance.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The comparison with traditional statistical and machine learning models gives a clear understanding of the LLM's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Reasoning Analysis:** Investigating the reasoning process of LLMs sheds light on the source of misalignment and how few-shot learning influences their predictions.
    *   **Addressing practicality:** The paper extends the application to a realistic sample design.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The study is based on data from Shanghai, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other contexts (different cultures, urban environments, transportation systems).
    *   **Model dependence:** The conclusion depends on the specific LLM used (DeepSeek-R1). Different LLMs could yield different results.
    *   **Sample size:** While the paper demonstrates improved results with a small number of examples, the overall sample is still relatively small (874 respondents). It remains a small sample size compared to traditional datasets (n ~ 1000s). More research is needed to determine the minimum required sample size for LLMs to achieve reliable results across different populations. The sample might also have a bias toward younger and higher income population, which might have impacted the results.
    *   **Parroting problems:** The paper could delve more deeply into the practical challenges of using LLMs, such as their proneness to hallucination (which the paper mentions but does not thoroughly address). This may impact the credibility and robustness of the study.
    *   **Complexity:** While interpretability is presented as an advantage, the paper does not show to what extent it is better or worse than other ML techniques.
    *   **Baseline implementation:** The paper's baseline implementation might not be the state-of-the-art implementations for LR and GBDT, which might have made them weaker than they should be.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on applying LLMs to transportation and urban planning.
    *   The findings provide a proof-of-concept for using LLMs to model travel behavior with limited data, which could have significant implications for data collection and analysis in the field.
    *   The study raises important questions about the behavioral alignment of LLMs and the need for careful validation of their predictions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has merit in its exploration of a new methodology (LLMs) within the travel behavior domain. The focus on behavioral alignment and the use of few-shot learning are valuable contributions. However, the limitations in generalizability (single city), the complexity of the technology, limited comparisons, and the relatively small sample size (compared to typical statistical studies) prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking study. It offers an interesting proof-of-concept and identifies important challenges, but more extensive validation is needed to establish its widespread applicability.

### Efficiently Access Diffusion Fisher: Within the Outer Product Span Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23264v1)
- **Authors**: Fangyikang Wang, Hubery Yin, Shaobin Zhuang, Huminhao Zhu, Yinan Li, Lei Qian, Chao Zhang, Hanbin Zhao, Hui Qian, Chen Li
- **Abstract**: Recent Diffusion models (DMs) advancements have explored incorporating the second-order diffusion Fisher information (DF), defined as the negative Hessian of log density, into various downstream tasks and theoretical analysis. However, current practices typically approximate the diffusion Fisher by applying auto-differentiation to the learned score network. This black-box method, though straightforward, lacks any accuracy guarantee and is time-consuming. In this paper, we show that the diffusion Fisher actually resides within a space spanned by the outer products of score and initial data. Based on the outer-product structure, we develop two efficient approximation algorithms to access the trace and matrix-vector multiplication of DF, respectively. These algorithms bypass the auto-differentiation operations with time-efficient vector-product calculations. Furthermore, we establish the approximation error bounds for the proposed algorithms. Experiments in likelihood evaluation and adjoint optimization demonstrate the superior accuracy and reduced computational cost of our proposed algorithms. Additionally, based on the novel outer-product formulation of DF, we design the first numerical verification experiment for the optimal transport property of the general PF-ODE deduced map.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the computational bottleneck of accessing the diffusion Fisher information (DF) in diffusion models (DMs).  Instead of relying on computationally expensive auto-differentiation of the learned score network, the authors demonstrate that the DF lies within a space spanned by the outer products of the score and initial data. Based on this "outer-product structure," they develop two efficient approximation algorithms for trace and matrix-vector multiplication of the DF.  The paper presents theoretical error bounds for these algorithms and demonstrates improved accuracy and reduced computational cost in likelihood evaluation and adjoint optimization tasks. A numerical experiment is included to verify the optimal transport property of diffusion ODEs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in identifying the outer-product structure of the diffusion Fisher information. This is a significant contribution because it allows for efficient approximation of DF without relying on auto-differentiation. Formulating the DF within a span space is insightful. The application of this structural finding to develop specific algorithms for trace and matrix-vector calculations is also novel, as is the numerical verification of the optimal transport property of the diffusion ODE.

*   **Significance:** The paper's contribution is significant for several reasons:

    *   **Efficiency:** The proposed algorithms significantly reduce the computational cost of accessing the DF, making it more practical for large-scale tasks like likelihood evaluation and adjoint optimization. This enables the efficient use of DFs for various downstream tasks related to DMs.
    *   **Accuracy:** By providing theoretical error bounds, the paper offers guarantees about the accuracy of the approximation, which is lacking in current auto-differentiation-based approaches.
    *   **Theoretical Insight:** The outer-product structure provides a deeper understanding of the properties of the diffusion Fisher information within DMs.
    *   **Practical Utility:** The method presents a clear way to improve several tasks that rely on the DF.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear theoretical derivation of the outer-product structure of the DF.
    *   The algorithms are well-defined and relatively easy to implement.
    *   The theoretical error bounds provide valuable guarantees about the approximation accuracy.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods in real-world tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the Dirac setting is a useful starting point, the general setting is more applicable to the range of current uses of diffusion models. It would improve this work if the algorithms could be shown to have the same characteristics in the general setting.
    *   The experiments, while convincing, could benefit from being on more complex datasets. Further analysis should be done to see if the performance of VJP can be improved with the help of Monte Carlo approximation.
    *   The numerical experiment verifying the OT property, while a good first step, relies on a rather coarse measure of asymmetry. Further analysis and experimentation could be done to see if the method can also be used with language models.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by enabling more efficient and accurate use of the diffusion Fisher information. It could lead to the development of new algorithms and techniques for various DM-related tasks and a greater understanding of these types of models.

*Score: 7.5*

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel perspective on the diffusion Fisher information and successfully leverages this perspective to develop efficient and accurate approximation algorithms.  The results are supported by theoretical analysis and experimental validation, but the scope of the experiments could be broader, and some aspects of the theoretical development are somewhat restricted. The work is likely to have a significant impact on the field, as it offers a practical solution to a computational bottleneck. The innovation isn't truly groundbreaking, but it is a solid, valuable contribution to the understanding of and practical work with Diffusion Models. This combination justifies the score assigned.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Image Aesthetic Reasoning: A New Benchmark for Medical Image Screening with MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23265v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Sun, Yi Wei, Long Yu
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are of great application across many domains, such as multimodal understanding and generation. With the development of diffusion models (DM) and unified MLLMs, the performance of image generation has been significantly improved, however, the study of image screening is rare and its performance with MLLMs is unsatisfactory due to the lack of data and the week image aesthetic reasoning ability in MLLMs. In this work, we propose a complete solution to address these problems in terms of data and methodology. For data, we collect a comprehensive medical image screening dataset with 1500+ samples, each sample consists of a medical image, four generated images, and a multiple-choice answer. The dataset evaluates the aesthetic reasoning ability under four aspects: \textit{(1) Appearance Deformation, (2) Principles of Physical Lighting and Shadow, (3) Placement Layout, (4) Extension Rationality}. For methodology, we utilize long chains of thought (CoT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization with Dynamic Proportional Accuracy reward, called DPA-GRPO, to enhance the image aesthetic reasoning ability of MLLMs. Our experimental results reveal that even state-of-the-art closed-source MLLMs, such as GPT-4o and Qwen-VL-Max, exhibit performance akin to random guessing in image aesthetic reasoning. In contrast, by leveraging the reinforcement learning approach, we are able to surpass the score of both large-scale models and leading closed-source models using a much smaller model. We hope our attempt on medical image screening will serve as a regular configuration in image aesthetic reasoning in the future.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new benchmark dataset and methodology for evaluating and improving the image aesthetic reasoning abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in the context of medical image screening. The authors collect a dataset of medical images along with generated variations exhibiting aesthetic flaws (Appearance Deformation, Lighting/Shadow issues, Layout problems, and Extension Rationality failures). They then propose a two-stage reinforcement learning approach called DPA-GRPO (Dynamic Proportional Accuracy Group Relative Policy Optimization) to enhance the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs. The results demonstrate that even state-of-the-art closed-source MLLMs perform poorly on this task, and that their DPA-GRPO approach significantly outperforms these models, even with a smaller model size.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel dataset tailored to a specific, practical application – medical image screening – which is a notable strength. The approach to address the task, DPA-GRPO, uses a combination of existing techniques (CoT, GRPO) but introduces the Dynamic Proportional Accuracy reward, which is a specific adaptation for multi-choice questions. The dataset is the more novel aspect.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a crucial gap in the capabilities of current MLLMs: their limited ability for image aesthetic reasoning, particularly in specialized domains like medical imaging. Addressing this limitation is significant because aesthetic flaws in generated or altered medical images could lead to misinterpretations or diagnostic errors. The proposed DPA-GRPO method shows promise in addressing this gap and improving the reliability of MLLMs in these critical applications. However, The significance hinges on whether image *aesthetic reasoning* as defined in the paper equates to improvements in real-world medical image screening. It needs to show improvements to medically-relevant outcomes.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The dataset fills a void for this specific application of image aesthetic reasoning within the medical domain.
    *   The DPA-GRPO approach demonstrates a practical way to improve MLLM performance in this area, even with smaller models.
    *   The paper clearly defines the four dimensions of aesthetic reasoning considered in the dataset, providing a useful framework.
    *   Extensive experiments are conducted to compare the performance of various MLLMs, providing a comprehensive analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The definition of "image aesthetics" is somewhat subjective and may not directly translate to clinical utility in medical image screening. The paper needs to justify why these aesthetic criteria are important in a *medical* setting. Does it lead to improved lesion detection? Does it prevent AI hallucinations?
    *   While the DPA-GRPO method shows improvement, it still requires a CoT dataset for pre-training, which can be costly to create.
    *   The experiments focus on a specific type of medical image generation scenario. The generalizability of the findings to other medical imaging modalities or applications might be limited.
    *   While the paper states models perform 'at the level of random guessing' further statistical tests would strenghten the claim.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research into developing more robust and reliable MLLMs for medical imaging applications. The dataset and methodology could serve as a valuable resource for benchmarking and improving the aesthetic reasoning capabilities of these models. It could also encourage further exploration of reinforcement learning techniques for fine-tuning MLLMs in specialized domains. However, its influence depends on how well the concept of "aesthetics" ties back to improvements on real medical screening tasks.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel dataset, addresses an important problem related to MLLMs in the medical domain, and proposes a promising method for improvement. The dataset and concept is novel. However, the significance is somewhat dampened by the reliance on somewhat subjective "aesthetic" criteria which may not directly related to relevant clinical outcomes. Without demonstrating clear improvements to real-world medical screening outcomes, the significance in the medical AI field is moderate. Finally, the novelty of the algorithmic contribution is limited.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Disrupting Vision-Language Model-Driven Navigation Services via Adversarial Object Fusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23266v1)
- **Authors**: Chunlong Xie, Jialing He, Shangwei Guo, Jiacheng Wang, Shudong Zhang, Tianwei Zhang, Tao Xiang
- **Abstract**: We present Adversarial Object Fusion (AdvOF), a novel attack framework targeting vision-and-language navigation (VLN) agents in service-oriented environments by generating adversarial 3D objects. While foundational models like Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision Language Models (VLMs) have enhanced service-oriented navigation systems through improved perception and decision-making, their integration introduces vulnerabilities in mission-critical service workflows. Existing adversarial attacks fail to address service computing contexts, where reliability and quality-of-service (QoS) are paramount. We utilize AdvOF to investigate and explore the impact of adversarial environments on the VLM-based perception module of VLN agents. In particular, AdvOF first precisely aggregates and aligns the victim object positions in both 2D and 3D space, defining and rendering adversarial objects. Then, we collaboratively optimize the adversarial object with regularization between the adversarial and victim object across physical properties and VLM perceptions. Through assigning importance weights to varying views, the optimization is processed stably and multi-viewedly by iterative fusions from local updates and justifications. Our extensive evaluations demonstrate AdvOF can effectively degrade agent performance under adversarial conditions while maintaining minimal interference with normal navigation tasks. This work advances the understanding of service security in VLM-powered navigation systems, providing computational foundations for robust service composition in physical-world deployments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Adversarial Object Fusion (AdvOF), a novel attack framework designed to disrupt vision-and-language navigation (VLN) agents operating in service-oriented environments.  AdvOF focuses on generating adversarial 3D objects that can mislead the perception module of VLN agents, causing them to misinterpret their surroundings and fail to follow instructions correctly. The framework comprises three main components: Aligned Object Rendering (aligning the victim object in both 2D and 3D space), Adversarial Collaborative Optimization (jointly matching features in 2D/3D), and Adversarial Object Fusion (iterative update and importance weighting for multi-view stability). The authors demonstrate that AdvOF can effectively degrade agent performance under adversarial conditions, while maintaining minimal interference with normal navigation tasks. They validate AdvOF's effectiveness across multiple VLN agents and datasets, showing its superiority compared to existing 2D and 3D attacks.  The work aims to advance the understanding of service security in VLM-powered navigation systems.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to attacking VLN agents.  The specific combination of aligned rendering, collaborative optimization, and multi-view fusion to generate 3D adversarial objects represents a significant advancement over existing 2D attacks (which often suffer from misalignment and multi-view inefficiencies) and 3D attacks (which often lack cross-modal awareness and struggle with the heterogeneity of VLMs). The emphasis on adversarial object generation as a means of disrupting service-oriented navigation workflows is a compelling and practical attack vector.  The method's explicit handling of multi-view consistency and the iterative fusion approach is also a unique contribution.

**Significance:** VLN agents are becoming increasingly important in various real-world applications, and their security vulnerabilities have significant implications. By demonstrating the effectiveness of AdvOF, the paper highlights a critical security concern and provides a pathway for further research into more robust VLN systems. The paper's findings emphasize the importance of considering adversarial attacks in the design and deployment of VLM-powered navigation systems. The experiments are thorough and compare the proposed attack with strong baselines and also perform various analysis which support the arguments of the paper.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly defines the problem of adversarial attacks on VLN agents within a service computing context.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:** AdvOF is a well-designed framework that addresses key challenges related to 3D adversarial manipulation, multi-view robustness, and cross-modal consistency.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of AdvOF and its superiority compared to existing attack methods. Extensive experiments validate the claims by evaluation with diverse VLN agents and datasets. Also, the transferability analysis and ablation studies also shows a comprehensive study of the proposed approach.
*   **Practical Implications:**  The paper has practical implications for the development of more secure and reliable VLN systems.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **White-box focus in primary evaluation:** While the paper mentions transferability and black-box implications, the core evaluation primarily focuses on a white-box setting (access to the agent's VLM). More extensive black-box testing would strengthen the paper's findings and highlight the real-world applicability of AdvOF.
*   **Limited Defenses:** The paper briefly touches on defenses. A more detailed exploration of potential defenses and the limitations of AdvOF in the face of these defenses would be a valuable addition. This addition would also open the scope for future research in the defense strategy and mechanisms.
*   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost associated with generating adversarial objects using AdvOF. The iterative optimization process and VLM queries could be computationally expensive, which might limit its scalability in certain applications.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of VLN by raising awareness of security vulnerabilities and providing a new approach to attacking VLN agents. It also provides a foundation for future research into more robust VLN systems that are resistant to adversarial attacks. The methodology can be extended into several other applications of VLN systems.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates strong novelty in its approach to attacking VLN agents with a well-designed framework. The experiments are thorough and highlight the effectiveness of the proposed approach as compared to a diverse set of baselines. The work is valuable because it emphasizes a critical security concern and offers a framework for generating more robust VLN systems; however, the primary focus of the experimentations is in a white-box setting which is a bit limiting. Also the paper can be more impactful with the addition of defense strategies. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified due to the paper's significant contributions and potential influence in the field, with a slight reduction because of the limitations mentioned above.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Does Machine Unlearning Truly Remove Model Knowledge? A Framework for Auditing Unlearning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23270v1)
- **Authors**: Haokun Chen, Yueqi Zhang, Yuan Bi, Yao Zhang, Tong Liu, Jinhe Bi, Jian Lan, Jindong Gu, Claudia Grosser, Denis Krompass, Nassir Navab, Volker Tresp
- **Abstract**: In recent years, Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable advancements, drawing significant attention from the research community. Their capabilities are largely attributed to large-scale architectures, which require extensive training on massive datasets. However, such datasets often contain sensitive or copyrighted content sourced from the public internet, raising concerns about data privacy and ownership. Regulatory frameworks, such as the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), grant individuals the right to request the removal of such sensitive information. This has motivated the development of machine unlearning algorithms that aim to remove specific knowledge from models without the need for costly retraining. Despite these advancements, evaluating the efficacy of unlearning algorithms remains a challenge due to the inherent complexity and generative nature of LLMs. In this work, we introduce a comprehensive auditing framework for unlearning evaluation, comprising three benchmark datasets, six unlearning algorithms, and five prompt-based auditing methods. By using various auditing algorithms, we evaluate the effectiveness and robustness of different unlearning strategies. To explore alternatives beyond prompt-based auditing, we propose a novel technique that leverages intermediate activation perturbations, addressing the limitations of auditing methods that rely solely on model inputs and outputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Does Machine Unlearning Truly Remove Model Knowledge? A Framework for Auditing Unlearning in LLMs" introduces a comprehensive auditing framework to evaluate the effectiveness and robustness of unlearning algorithms in Large Language Models (LLMs).  The framework consists of three benchmark datasets (WHP, TOFU, RWKU), six representative unlearning algorithms (GA, GD, KD, RT, DPO, NPO), and five prompt-based auditing strategies (AOA, MASK, ICL, GCG, SoftGCG).  Additionally, the authors propose a novel auditing technique called Activation Perturbation-based Auditing (ActPert) which perturbs intermediate model activations to detect residual traces of forgotten information. They conduct extensive experiments using this framework and provide an in-depth analysis of the limitations of current unlearning algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The creation of a standardized auditing framework is a valuable contribution.  The inclusion of multiple datasets, unlearning algorithms, and auditing techniques allows for a more thorough and robust evaluation compared to studies that focus on only one or two methods.  This addresses a crucial need in the unlearning field which has, until now, suffered from a lack of standardized evaluation protocols.
    *   **Novel Auditing Technique (ActPert):** The introduction of ActPert is a notable strength. Moving beyond solely prompt-based auditing offers a way to probe the internal representations of the LLM and potentially uncover residual knowledge that might be missed by surface-level checks. The technique is grounded in previous work in activation engineering, adding a solid foundation to the approach.
    *   **Empirical Analysis:** The paper provides an extensive empirical analysis, comparing the performance of different unlearning algorithms under various auditing conditions. The detailed results, including observations regarding the efficacy of different methods across datasets and the impact of parameters like dataset size and model architecture, provide valuable insights for researchers in the field.
    *   **Identifies Limitations:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing unlearning algorithms and auditing methods. Highlighting the fragility of unlearning to prompt engineering attacks and the difficulty in completely removing pretraining knowledge are important contributions to the field.
    *   **Open Sourcing:** The stated intention to open-source the framework is a significant positive, encouraging further research and development in this area. This makes the framework accessible and readily usable by other researchers.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **ActPert Specificity:** While ActPert is a novel idea, its effectiveness doesn't consistently surpass the baselines across all scenarios.  The performance seems highly dependent on the specific dataset and unlearning algorithm used. Further analysis is needed to understand *why* ActPert works better in certain contexts and how it can be optimized for broader applicability.
    *   **Benchmark limitations:** Although the provided benchmarks are useful, the number of datasets remains relatively low. The conclusions about each unlearning and evaluation method efficacy will require further validation on other datasets. Also, TOFU dataset's reliance on synthetic data, while offering a direct access to ground-truth queries, it might not reflect all the complexities found in real-world applications.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Guarantees:** The paper is primarily empirical and doesn't provide any theoretical guarantees about the effectiveness of the proposed auditing methods or the unlearning algorithms themselves. While empirical evaluation is important, theoretical justification can provide more confidence in the robustness and reliability of the methods.
    *   **Limited scope of Unlearning Targets:** The work focuses on unlearning specific individual or character-related information. The effectiveness of the presented framework in auditing more complex or abstract knowledge removal is not addressed.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a timely and important problem: verifying the effectiveness of machine unlearning in LLMs. As LLMs are increasingly used in sensitive applications, ensuring that data removal requests are properly handled is crucial for privacy and legal compliance.
    *   The proposed framework provides a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners to evaluate and compare different unlearning algorithms.
    *   The identification of limitations in existing unlearning algorithms and auditing methods can guide future research efforts.

**Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I assign this paper a score of **7**.

**Rationale:**

The paper's primary strength lies in its construction of a comprehensive, standardized auditing framework for evaluating unlearning algorithms in LLMs, and the novel ActPert auditing method.  This addresses a clear gap in the existing literature and provides a valuable resource for the research community. The extensive empirical analysis provides useful insights, and the identified limitations help to guide future research directions.

However, the effectiveness of the proposed ActPert method is not universally superior, and its performance requires further investigation. While the benchmarks are helpful, their quantity and representativeness could be expanded. The lack of theoretical guarantees is also a limitation, reducing the level of confidence in the long-term reliability of the methods.  Also, while comprehensive, it does not address abstract concepts and harmful instructions as unlearning targets.

Overall, the paper presents a significant contribution to the field of machine unlearning, but requires further refinement and theoretical grounding to fully realize its potential. The open sourcing will potentially elevate its impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Wireless Agentic AI with Retrieval-Augmented Multimodal Semantic Perception
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23275v1)
- **Authors**: Guangyuan Liu, Yinqiu Liu, Ruichen Zhang, Hongyang Du, Dusit Niyato, Zehui Xiong, Sumei Sun, Abbas Jamalipour
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of multimodal AI and Large Language Models (LLMs) has greatly enhanced real-time interaction, decision-making, and collaborative tasks. However, in wireless multi-agent scenarios, limited bandwidth poses significant challenges to exchanging semantically rich multimodal information efficiently. Traditional semantic communication methods, though effective, struggle with redundancy and loss of crucial details. To overcome these challenges, we propose a Retrieval-Augmented Multimodal Semantic Communication (RAMSemCom) framework. RAMSemCom incorporates iterative, retrieval-driven semantic refinement tailored for distributed multi-agent environments, enabling efficient exchange of critical multimodal elements through local caching and selective transmission. Our approach dynamically optimizes retrieval using deep reinforcement learning (DRL) to balance semantic fidelity with bandwidth constraints. A comprehensive case study on multi-agent autonomous driving demonstrates that our DRL-based retrieval strategy significantly improves task completion efficiency and reduces communication overhead compared to baseline methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RAMSemCom, a retrieval-augmented multimodal semantic communication framework specifically designed for wireless multi-agent systems with bandwidth limitations. The framework leverages Retrieval-Augmented Perception (RAP) and Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) to enable efficient exchange of crucial multimodal elements (visual, textual, auditory) among agents. It incorporates an iterative, retrieval-driven semantic refinement mechanism, optimized using DRL to dynamically balance semantic fidelity with bandwidth constraints through local caching and selective transmission.  A case study in multi-agent autonomous driving demonstrates that RAMSemCom significantly improves task completion efficiency and reduces communication overhead compared to baseline methods. The paper concludes with future directions including federated semantic learning, adaptive semantic compression, cross-modality retrieval optimization and scalability evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the integration of RAP principles into a distributed semantic communication framework, specifically tailored for bandwidth-constrained multi-agent scenarios. While RAG/RAP is increasingly common in NLP and some vision tasks, adapting it for *communication efficiency* in *resource-constrained* multi-agent settings is less explored. The DRL-based optimization of retrieval decisions further contributes to the novelty, allowing dynamic adaptation to channel conditions and semantic needs. The demonstration within the autonomous driving domain also adds a layer of practical relevance.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing need for efficient communication in distributed AI systems. The presented framework has potential to address bandwidth limitations in various applications like collaborative robotics, vehicular networks, and IoT deployments. By reducing the required bandwidth for semantic information exchange, the framework facilitates scalability and real-time operation in such scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** Clearly articulates the challenges of multimodal communication in bandwidth-constrained multi-agent systems.
    *   **Technically sound:** Combines existing techniques (RAP, DRL) in a novel and effective manner.
    *   **Demonstrated improvement:** The case study provides empirical evidence of RAMSemCom's advantages over baseline approaches.
    *   **Clear explanations:** The framework's components and their interaction are well-explained and clearly presented.
    *   **Discussion of future directions:** Demonstrates awareness of potential for further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Case Study:** The case study focuses solely on autonomous driving with simulated data from the MAPLM dataset. Demonstrating its effectiveness in other multi-agent scenarios (e.g., swarm robotics, cooperative sensing) would strengthen the claims of general applicability.
    *   **Complexity:** The framework introduces significant complexity, requiring a DRL-based scheduler and careful design of semantic retrieval mechanisms. The practical deployment overhead and training costs need to be carefully considered.
    *   **Baseline Choice:**  While the baselines provide a comparative context, evaluating against more advanced semantic communication techniques (if available) would be preferable. The heuristic baseline seems quite simple, potentially overstating the gains.
    *   **Scalability Discussion:** The paper mentions scalability in future directions but doesn't provide any analysis or empirical assessment of its scaling behavior with a large number of agents or more complex environments.
    *   **Implementation details:** More implementation details regarding the semantic encoder/decoder architectures, the feature extraction methods used for similarity search, and DRL environment setup would improve reproducibility and allow others to build upon the work.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   Can enable more scalable and efficient distributed AI systems in various application domains.
    *   Provides a valuable framework for future research in retrieval-augmented semantic communication.
    *   The DRL-based optimization strategy can inspire new approaches for resource allocation in wireless AI systems.

*   **Overall:**

    The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of semantic communication and multi-agent AI.  The integration of RAG/RAP principles with DRL for efficient resource allocation represents a novel approach to a relevant and important problem. While the scope of the case study and the complexity of the framework are limitations, the potential impact is significant.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper earns a 7.5 due to its novelty in adapting RAP to multi-agent communication and its significance in addressing bandwidth constraints. However, the limited empirical validation (focusing solely on autonomous driving and using a simulation environment) and the lack of a detailed scalability analysis temper the score. A more comprehensive evaluation across different application domains and an in-depth discussion of the framework's complexity overhead would have pushed the score higher.

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Arabic AI Fingerprint: Stylometric Analysis and Detection of Large Language Models Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23276v1)
- **Authors**: Maged S. Al-Shaibani, Moataz Ahmed
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved unprecedented capabilities in generating human-like text, posing subtle yet significant challenges for information integrity across critical domains, including education, social media, and academia, enabling sophisticated misinformation campaigns, compromising healthcare guidance, and facilitating targeted propaganda. This challenge becomes severe, particularly in under-explored and low-resource languages like Arabic. This paper presents a comprehensive investigation of Arabic machine-generated text, examining multiple generation strategies (generation from the title only, content-aware generation, and text refinement) across diverse model architectures (ALLaM, Jais, Llama, and GPT-4) in academic, and social media domains. Our stylometric analysis reveals distinctive linguistic patterns differentiating human-written from machine-generated Arabic text across these varied contexts. Despite their human-like qualities, we demonstrate that LLMs produce detectable signatures in their Arabic outputs, with domain-specific characteristics that vary significantly between different contexts. Based on these insights, we developed BERT-based detection models that achieved exceptional performance in formal contexts (up to 99.9\% F1-score) with strong precision across model architectures. Our cross-domain analysis confirms generalization challenges previously reported in the literature. To the best of our knowledge, this work represents the most comprehensive investigation of Arabic machine-generated text to date, uniquely combining multiple prompt generation methods, diverse model architectures, and in-depth stylometric analysis across varied textual domains, establishing a foundation for developing robust, linguistically-informed detection systems essential for preserving information integrity in Arabic-language contexts.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper and can provide a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the detection of machine-generated Arabic text produced by large language models (LLMs). It focuses on Arabic, a language often under-represented in NLP research. The authors generate Arabic text using various methods (title-only, content-aware, text refinement) and diverse LLM architectures (ALLaM, Jais, Llama, GPT-4). The study performs a stylometric analysis to identify linguistic differences between human-written and machine-generated Arabic text in academic and social media domains. Based on this analysis, they develop BERT-based detection models. The paper examines cross-model generalization and multi-class LLM identification and finds high performance on academic writing but limitations on social media content, indicating domain-specific challenges. The work emphasizes the importance of robust detection systems in Arabic to preserve information integrity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper has several aspects of novelty:

*   **Focus on Arabic:**  The primary novelty is the in-depth investigation of machine-generated *Arabic* text detection. While English has been extensively studied, Arabic, with its unique linguistic features and lower resource availability, receives less attention.  This language-specific focus is a significant contribution.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:**  The study combines multiple generation strategies (prompts), diverse LLM architectures (including Arabic-specific models like ALLaM and Jais), and covers different domains (academic and social media). This multifaceted approach is a strength, allowing for a more holistic understanding of the challenges.
*   **Stylometric Analysis:** The stylometric analysis systematically investigates linguistic patterns differentiating human and machine-generated text in Arabic, which helps inform detector development and provides a deep dive into the problem's nature.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a significant and growing problem: the proliferation of machine-generated text and its potential impact on information integrity, academic integrity, and the spread of misinformation.

*   **Practical Implications:**  The development of effective detection models for Arabic has practical implications for educational institutions, social media platforms, and news organizations that need to verify the authenticity of Arabic content.
*   **Insights into LLM Behavior:**  The stylometric analysis provides valuable insights into the linguistic characteristics of LLM-generated Arabic text, which can help guide the development of more robust and human-like generation models.
*   **Identification of Challenges:** The study identifies crucial challenges, such as cross-domain generalization and the detection of sophisticated generation techniques (e.g., text refinement/polishing) in a resource-limited language.
*   **Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of its detection models in social media contexts, which highlights the complexity of detecting machine-generated text in less formal and more nuanced styles. It’s also worth noting that while using BERT based architecture offers good accuracy, it's not computationally efficient and not applicable in real-time scenarios.

**Strengths:**

*   Thorough experimental design with multiple LLMs, generation methods, and domains.
*   Detailed stylometric analysis provides valuable insights.
*   Development and evaluation of BERT-based detection models.
*   Clear articulation of the problem's importance and challenges.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The limitations in social media detection are significant and require further research.
*   The reliance on BERT architecture, while effective, introduces concerns regarding computational efficiency for real-world application.
*   The paper acknowledges data bias which is typical for all model which can lead to inaccurate output for various context.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable and timely contribution to the field of NLP, particularly in the context of Arabic language processing. The combination of comprehensive experiments, insightful analysis, and the development of detection models makes this a significant work. The limitations regarding social media detection and computational efficiency highlight areas for future research, but do not diminish the paper's overall impact. Considering novelty and practical value, I assign a score of:

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sentinel: Attention Probing of Proxy Models for LLM Context Compression with an Understanding Perspective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23277v1)
- **Authors**: Yong Zhang, Yanwen Huang, Ning Cheng, Yang Guo, Yun Zhu, Yanmeng Wang, Shaojun Wang, Jing Xiao
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances large language models (LLMs) with external context, but retrieved passages are often lengthy, noisy, or exceed input limits. Existing compression methods typically require supervised training of dedicated compression models, increasing cost and reducing portability. We propose Sentinel, a lightweight sentence-level compression framework that reframes context filtering as an attention-based understanding task. Rather than training a compression model, Sentinel probes decoder attention from an off-the-shelf 0.5B proxy LLM using a lightweight classifier to identify sentence relevance. Empirically, we find that query-context relevance estimation is consistent across model scales, with 0.5B proxies closely matching the behaviors of larger models. On the LongBench benchmark, Sentinel achieves up to 5$\times$ compression while matching the QA performance of 7B-scale compression systems. Our results suggest that probing native attention signals enables fast, effective, and question-aware context compression. Code available at: https://github.com/yzhangchuck/Sentinel.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Sentinel: Attention Probing of Proxy Models for LLM Context Compression with an Understanding Perspective":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sentinel, a novel lightweight framework for context compression in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. Sentinel reframes context filtering as an attention-based understanding task, probing the decoder attention of a small, off-the-shelf proxy LLM (0.5B) to identify sentence relevance.  Instead of training a dedicated compression model, Sentinel uses a simple logistic regression classifier to predict sentence relevance based on aggregated attention features. The authors demonstrate that query-context relevance estimation is relatively stable across model scales, allowing a small proxy to effectively mimic the behavior of larger models.  Experiments on LongBench show Sentinel achieves significant compression (up to 5x) while maintaining or even exceeding the QA performance of larger compression systems using 7B-scale models,  and generalizes well across different tasks, languages, and LLM backbones without task-specific tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its attention probing approach. It's a clever idea to leverage the inherent understanding capabilities embedded in LLM attention mechanisms for context compression, rather than relying on supervised training of a dedicated compression model. It's less common to use probing on proxy LLMs as a strategy for efficient context compression. While probing and attention mechanisms in LLMs have been explored extensively, the specific application to context compression in RAG, with a focus on lightweight and model-agnostic implementation using a small proxy LLM, contributes to the field. Reframing compression as an "understanding" problem, rather than a generation or reranking problem, is also a valuable conceptual shift.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is substantial. Context compression is a critical bottleneck in RAG pipelines, and the simplicity and efficiency of Sentinel address this directly. The fact that it can match or outperform much larger compression models while using a tiny proxy LLM is significant. This can potentially democratize access to RAG by reducing computational costs associated with context processing. The model-agnostic nature and zero-shot generalization capability also make it highly practical for real-world deployment. The findings on relevance stability across LLM sizes opens a promising pathway for optimizing RAG efficiency.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficiency:**  Sentinel's use of a small proxy model and lightweight classifier makes it computationally efficient compared to methods that require training larger compression models or performing extensive generation/reranking.
    *   **Model Agnostic:**  The method doesn't require task-specific fine-tuning, making it immediately usable with various LLMs and tasks.
    *   **Strong Performance:**  Experimental results on LongBench are compelling, showing competitive or superior performance to larger compression models in several scenarios.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written, clearly explaining the approach and experimental setup. The ablation studies are thorough.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Format Sensitivity:** The limitations related to format sensitivity in Few-Shot and Code Completion tasks are a valid concern. The approach's reliance on generic sentence segmentation can disrupt the delicate structure of these tasks. This limits the scope of problems Sentinel can immediately address.
    *   **Proxy Model Scope:** While the paper demonstrates results with Qwen models, it acknowledges the lack of cross-architecture evaluations. The extent to which attention patterns are consistently "probe-able" across different families like LLaMA or GPT requires further investigation.
    *   **Limited Evaluation Backbones:** The evaluation is limited to GPT-3.5-Turbo and Qwen-2.5-7B. While this is reasonable, more diverse LLM backbones are necessary to establish broad applicability.
    *   **Dependence on Probing Data:** The probing requires an external QA dataset, even if small. While the dataset is agnostic to the task the compressed context will be used on, it's still a pre-requisite that might be inconvenient in some scenarios.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Modes:**  The paper could benefit from a deeper analysis of where Sentinel fails and why. Identifying the specific types of questions or contexts where Sentinel's performance degrades would be valuable for future improvements.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    Sentinel presents a significant advance in context compression, primarily through its elegant use of attention probing with proxy models and the demonstration of relevance stability across LLM scales. Its efficiency and model-agnostic nature are substantial strengths. However, its acknowledged format sensitivity and reliance on external data, albeit QA-agnostic, for probing do limit its immediate applicability in certain contexts. A score of 7 reflects the core innovation, the demonstrably promising performance in RAG settings, and the clear path for future improvements that could expand the scope of its applicability and address existing limitations.

### MathArena: Evaluating LLMs on Uncontaminated Math Competitions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23281v1)
- **Authors**: Mislav Balunović, Jasper Dekoninck, Ivo Petrov, Nikola Jovanović, Martin Vechev
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of reasoning capabilities in large language models (LLMs) has led to notable improvements on mathematical benchmarks. However, many of the most commonly used evaluation datasets (e.g., AIME 2024) are widely available online, making it difficult to disentangle genuine reasoning from potential memorization. Furthermore, these benchmarks do not evaluate proof-writing capabilities, which are crucial for many mathematical tasks. To address this, we introduce MathArena, a new benchmark based on the following key insight: recurring math competitions provide a stream of high-quality, challenging problems that can be used for real-time evaluation of LLMs. By evaluating models as soon as new problems are released, we effectively eliminate the risk of contamination. Using this framework, we find strong signs of contamination in AIME 2024. Nonetheless, evaluations on harder competitions, such as SMT 2025 -- published well after model release dates -- demonstrate impressive reasoning capabilities in top-performing models. MathArena is also the first benchmark for proof-writing capabilities. On USAMO 2025, even top models score below 25%, far behind their performance on final-answer tasks. So far, we have evaluated 30 models across five competitions, totaling 149 problems. As an evolving benchmark, MathArena will continue to track the progress of LLMs on newly released competitions, ensuring rigorous and up-to-date evaluation of mathematical reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MATHARENA, a new benchmark for evaluating the mathematical reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). The key innovation is to use problems from newly released math competitions to avoid data contamination, a significant issue with existing benchmarks. MATHARENA includes both final-answer and proof-based problems, offering a more comprehensive evaluation of LLMs. The authors evaluated several state-of-the-art models on MATHARENA and found evidence of contamination in existing benchmarks (specifically AIME 2024) while also demonstrating impressive reasoning capabilities in top-performing models on harder, uncontaminated competitions. The benchmark and evaluation pipeline are publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its approach to mitigating data contamination by using problems from newly released competitions. This is a clever and timely solution to a growing problem in the field. Furthermore, the inclusion of proof-based problems is a valuable addition to existing benchmarks, which predominantly focus on final answers. This facilitates a more in-depth assessment of mathematical reasoning.
*   **Significance:** The MATHARENA benchmark addresses a key challenge in evaluating LLMs, namely, ensuring the integrity and reliability of the assessment. The evidence presented for contamination in AIME 2024 is compelling, highlighting the importance of the proposed methodology. By providing a transparent, reproducible, and continuously updated benchmark, MATHARENA contributes to more accurate and reliable evaluations of LLMs in the domain of mathematical reasoning. The benchmark also allows tracking of progress in LLM capabilities over time in a less biased fashion. It pushes for evaluations on the hardest reasoning challenges, such as proof generation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear motivation and well-defined problem statement (data contamination).
    *   Innovative solution leveraging newly released math competitions.
    *   Inclusion of proof-based problems for more comprehensive evaluation.
    *   Open-source benchmark and evaluation pipeline for transparency and reproducibility.
    *   Empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relatively small size of the benchmark, which can lead to wider confidence intervals in the results. This issue is, to some extent, unavoidable given the reliance on newly released competitions.
    *   Dependence on human graders for proof-based problems, which can be time-consuming and potentially introduce subjectivity (although the paper mitigates this with dual grading and justifications).
    *   The paper admits saturation of multiple-choice question format will limit benchmark use, and need for more difficult tests.
*   **Potential Influence:**
    MATHARENA has the potential to become a widely adopted benchmark for evaluating LLMs on mathematical reasoning, especially as concerns about data contamination continue to grow. Its public availability and continuous updates should help to ensure its relevance and usefulness over time. However, the small size of each test may result in low-powered analyses.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While MATHARENA isn't a complete paradigm shift, it introduces a critical methodological refinement needed to provide valid and reproducible mathematical reasoning research. The focus on newly released problems is strategically effective in the fight against data contamination, an increasingly salient issue with existing benchmarks. The benchmark has already gained visibility within the community. That being said, the size of the benchmark and the need for human grading in some cases may limit adoption.

### RSFAKE-1M: A Large-Scale Dataset for Detecting Diffusion-Generated Remote Sensing Forgeries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23283v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihong Tan, Jiayi Wang, Huiying Shi, Binyuan Huang, Hongchen Wei, Zhenzhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Detecting forged remote sensing images is becoming increasingly critical, as such imagery plays a vital role in environmental monitoring, urban planning, and national security. While diffusion models have emerged as the dominant paradigm for image generation, their impact on remote sensing forgery detection remains underexplored. Existing benchmarks primarily target GAN-based forgeries or focus on natural images, limiting progress in this critical domain. To address this gap, we introduce RSFAKE-1M, a large-scale dataset of 500K forged and 500K real remote sensing images. The fake images are generated by ten diffusion models fine-tuned on remote sensing data, covering six generation conditions such as text prompts, structural guidance, and inpainting. This paper presents the construction of RSFAKE-1M along with a comprehensive experimental evaluation using both existing detectors and unified baselines. The results reveal that diffusion-based remote sensing forgeries remain challenging for current methods, and that models trained on RSFAKE-1M exhibit notably improved generalization and robustness. Our findings underscore the importance of RSFAKE-1M as a foundation for developing and evaluating next-generation forgery detection approaches in the remote sensing domain. The dataset and other supplementary materials are available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/TZHSW/RSFAKE/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RSFAKE-1M, a large-scale dataset designed for detecting diffusion-generated forgeries in remote sensing images. The dataset comprises 500,000 forged images generated by ten different diffusion models fine-tuned on remote sensing data, covering six generation conditions (text prompts, segmentation masks, edge maps, vector maps, and inpainting). It also includes 500,000 real remote sensing images sampled from the fMoW dataset to ensure a balanced evaluation. The paper presents the dataset construction process, a comprehensive experimental evaluation using existing detectors and unified baselines, and demonstrates that diffusion-based remote sensing forgeries remain challenging. It also highlights the improved generalization and robustness of models trained on RSFAKE-1M.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the scale and diversity of the dataset, specifically targeting diffusion model-generated forgeries in remote sensing. Existing remote sensing forgery datasets are smaller and primarily focus on GAN-generated images. The comprehensive evaluation across different generation conditions and models is also a strength.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in the remote sensing forgery detection domain.  With the increasing sophistication of diffusion models, traditional forgery detection techniques are becoming less effective.  A benchmark like RSFAKE-1M is necessary to drive the development of more robust and generalizable detectors. The paper's experiments clearly demonstrate the limitations of existing methods and the potential benefits of training on the new dataset. The results reveal a compelling argument for the need for such a dataset. The scale of the data set contributes to reducing bias in training machine learning models for remote sensing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Large scale and high diversity of forgery types.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation.
    *   Clear demonstration of the challenges posed by diffusion model forgeries.
    *   The models the team used are readily available and accessible to the public.
    *   The paper presents the processes that were used to address potential bias.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper evaluates a good range of existing detectors, further investigation into fine-tuning SOTA methods on the dataset would strengthen the analysis and give the reader a more solid benchmark.

*   **Impact:** RSFAKE-1M is likely to have a significant impact on the remote sensing forgery detection field. It will serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners, enabling the development and evaluation of more effective forgery detection techniques. The benchmark's scale, diversity, and targeted focus on diffusion models will drive progress in this critical area. The data set could be expanded in the future to cover a wider-array of environmental impacts due to remote sensing forgeries, leading to a more accurate evaluation of potential risks.

**Justification:**

The paper's contribution is significant because it provides a much-needed resource for a domain that is increasingly vulnerable to sophisticated forgeries. The scale and diversity of the dataset, combined with a comprehensive evaluation, make it a valuable asset for the remote sensing and computer vision communities. While there is always room for improvement, such as further analysis using fine-tuned SOTA methods, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The potential impact on improving the security and reliability of remote sensing data justifies a high score.

### How Does Response Length Affect Long-Form Factuality
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23295v1)
- **Authors**: James Xu Zhao, Jimmy Z. J. Liu, Bryan Hooi, See-Kiong Ng
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are widely used for long-form text generation. However, factual errors in the responses would undermine their reliability. Despite growing attention to LLM factuality, the effect of response length on factuality remains underexplored. In this work, we systematically investigate this relationship by first introducing an automatic and bi-level long-form factuality evaluation framework, which achieves high agreement with human annotations while being cost-effective. Using this framework, we conduct controlled experiments and find that longer responses exhibit lower factual precision, confirming the presence of length bias. To explain this phenomenon, we empirically examine three hypotheses: error propagation, long context, and facts exhaustion. Our results reveal that facts exhaustion, where the model gradually exhausts more reliable knowledge, is the primary cause of factual degradation, rather than the other two hypotheses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the relationship between response length and factuality in long-form text generation by Large Language Models (LLMs).  It introduces BAFE (Bi-level Atomic Fact Evaluation), a new automatic factuality evaluation framework that decomposes responses into atomic facts and verifies them against Wikipedia and Google Search. Using BAFE, the authors conduct controlled experiments demonstrating that longer responses exhibit lower factual precision, confirming the existence of a length bias.  They then explore three hypotheses to explain this phenomenon: error propagation, long context effects, and facts exhaustion. Their findings suggest that facts exhaustion, where the model gradually exhausts reliable knowledge and resorts to less certain details, is the primary driver of factual degradation as response length increases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a practically relevant issue in LLM research: the impact of response length on factuality. While the general concern about LLM hallucinations is well-known, the *systematic* investigation of length bias and the isolation of *facts exhaustion* as the dominant cause contributes a novel perspective.  Previous works have hinted at this relationship, but this work provides a more rigorous and controlled analysis. The introduction of BAFE as a more efficient and accurate evaluation framework is also a positive contribution, though it builds on existing atomic fact-checking methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have significant implications for the development and deployment of LLMs for long-form text generation. Understanding the causes of factual degradation allows for the design of targeted mitigation strategies.  The identification of facts exhaustion suggests that approaches focused on knowledge retrieval or structured generation may be more effective than simply increasing model size or training data. Also, the more efficient and accurate evaluation framework (BAFE) would contribute significantly to the future research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The authors use carefully controlled experiments to isolate the effect of response length and to test their hypotheses.  The use of consistent prompts and specific instructions to control response length is commendable.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper considers multiple hypotheses and provides empirical evidence to support or refute each. The combination of statistical analysis (autocorrelation) and counterfactual analysis strengthens their claims.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The findings are relevant to real-world applications of LLMs where long-form generation is required.
    *   **Improved Evaluation Framework:** BAFE seems like a significant step forward from the existing methods by introducing a simple yet effective bi-level verification framework with a lower cost.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Dependence:** The experiments are primarily conducted using GPT-4o. While this is a powerful and widely used model, the generalizability of the findings to other architectures and training paradigms (e.g., open-source LLMs) is not fully established.
    *   **Task Specificity:** The study focuses on biography generation and long fact description tasks. While these are relevant, it's possible that the facts exhaustion phenomenon may manifest differently in other generation tasks (e.g., creative writing, summarization).
    *   **Facts Exhaustion Metric:** Though facts exhaustion is identified as the most significant factor, it doesn’t have a rigorous measurement.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM factuality and long-form generation. The findings could lead to the development of new techniques for mitigating length bias, such as retrieval-augmented generation or knowledge-guided generation. The BAFE framework could be adopted by other researchers for evaluating factuality.
*   **Overall:** The paper is a solid contribution to the field. It has novelty and provides some meaningful insights on an important challenge. It has rigor in the methods it uses, but I believe that it lacks generalizability due to its model dependent experiment. The findings could be improved by having a more rigorous metrics for measurement.

### EmoBench-UA: A Benchmark Dataset for Emotion Detection in Ukrainian
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23297v1)
- **Authors**: Daryna Dementieva, Nikolay Babakov, Alexander Fraser
- **Abstract**: While Ukrainian NLP has seen progress in many texts processing tasks, emotion classification remains an underexplored area with no publicly available benchmark to date. In this work, we introduce EmoBench-UA, the first annotated dataset for emotion detection in Ukrainian texts. Our annotation schema is adapted from the previous English-centric works on emotion detection (Mohammad et al., 2018; Mohammad, 2022) guidelines. The dataset was created through crowdsourcing using the Toloka.ai platform ensuring high-quality of the annotation process. Then, we evaluate a range of approaches on the collected dataset, starting from linguistic-based baselines, synthetic data translated from English, to large language models (LLMs). Our findings highlight the challenges of emotion classification in non-mainstream languages like Ukrainian and emphasize the need for further development of Ukrainian-specific models and training resources.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EmoBench-UA, the first publicly available benchmark dataset for emotion detection in Ukrainian texts. The authors describe the crowdsourcing annotation pipeline, which leveraged the Toloka.ai platform and incorporated several quality control mechanisms. They evaluate a range of classification approaches, including linguistic-based baselines, Transformer-based encoders, translation into English, and prompting large language models (LLMs). The results highlight the challenges of emotion classification in Ukrainian and emphasize the need for language-specific models and resources. The dataset and top-performing model are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty is the creation of the EmoBench-UA dataset itself. While emotion detection is a well-established task, the lack of a publicly available benchmark for Ukrainian is a clear gap the paper addresses. The dataset's annotation pipeline, involving crowdsourcing and quality control, is a good contribution to data creation best practices. The evaluation of various models, while not groundbreaking in itself, provides valuable insights into their performance on Ukrainian texts.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in enabling further research in Ukrainian NLP, particularly in the under-explored area of emotion detection. EmoBench-UA provides a foundation for developing and evaluating Ukrainian-specific models.  The results showing the performance of various approaches can guide future researchers in prioritizing certain model types and techniques. The release of the dataset and model fosters reproducibility and further advancements in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Creation:** The meticulous annotation process, including data filtering, crowdsourcing with quality control, and multiple annotators, is a major strength.  The high Krippendorff's alpha indicates strong inter-annotator agreement and reliability.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper evaluates a wide range of methods, from simple baselines to state-of-the-art LLMs, providing a good overview of the task's difficulty and the potential of different approaches.
    *   **Public Availability:** The release of both the dataset and the top-performing model is a major plus, enabling other researchers to build upon this work.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The authors address ethical implications, including fair compensation, data anonymization, and the potential for bias.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Emotion Set:**  The focus on Ekman's basic emotions, while common, might not capture the full complexity of human emotions expressed in Ukrainian texts.  More nuanced or implicit emotions were excluded.
    *   **Dataset Imbalance:** The dataset is imbalanced, which could bias the model evaluations.  The authors acknowledge this, but it's a limitation that future work could address.
    *   **LLM Prompting Strategies:** While LLMs are tested, the prompting strategies may not be fully optimized. The instructions are relatively simple and could benefit from further refinement. More careful few-shot demonstrations or more complex instruction following may provide better results.
    *   **Translation Issues:** The reliance on machine translation for both data selection and creating synthetic training data introduces potential noise and bias.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely become a key reference for anyone working on emotion detection in Ukrainian. It establishes a baseline for future research and provides a valuable resource for model development and evaluation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a significant and novel contribution with the creation of the EmoBench-UA dataset. It establishes a crucial foundation for emotion detection research in Ukrainian, a language that has been comparatively under-resourced in the NLP field. While the model evaluations are relatively standard, they offer valuable insights into the performance of different approaches on this specific language. The thorough dataset creation process and the public availability of both the dataset and top model underscore the paper's practicality and its potential for long-term impact. The ethical considerations section enhances the credibility and responsible nature of the work.

While the paper does have weaknesses, such as the limitations on emotion types and LLM prompting strategies, these limitations are clearly acknowledged and do not detract significantly from the overall value of the contribution. This paper fills a key gap in research on Ukrainian NLP, and thus merits a high score.
