- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ATLAS: Learning to Optimally Memorize the Context at Test Time":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ATLAS, a novel long-term memory module designed to improve the performance of sequence models in long-context understanding.  It addresses limitations in existing recurrent models, such as online-only memory updates, limited memory capacity, and less expressive memory management. ATLAS incorporates: (1) a sliding window update rule (Omega rule) that optimizes memory based on a local context window rather than just the last token, (2) higher-order feature mappings (e.g., polynomial kernels) to increase memory capacity, and (3) the Muon optimizer for more effective memory management via approximate second-order information. The authors also present a new family of Transformer-like architectures called DEEPTRANSFORMERS that generalizes the original Transformer. Experiments on various benchmarks show ATLAS outperforms Transformers and recent linear recurrent models in language modeling, common-sense reasoning, and long-context understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core contributions of the paper—the Omega rule, the integration of higher-order feature mappings with deep memory modules, and the application of the Muon optimizer to memory management within recurrent architectures—demonstrate significant novelty. While the individual components might have precedents in isolation, their combination and application within the ATLAS framework offer a fresh approach to addressing the long-context problem. The DEEPTRANSFORMERS architecture, as a generalization of Transformers, is a compelling theoretical contribution. However, the novelty somewhat relies on existing architectural choices like the selection of a MLP which could be done through another architecture.
*   **Significance:** The quadratic complexity bottleneck of traditional attention mechanisms remains a critical challenge in scaling language models. ATLAS directly tackles this limitation by offering a more efficient recurrent architecture that can maintain performance on long-context tasks. The empirical results clearly show ATLAS's superiority over existing methods, particularly in ultra-long sequence tasks, indicating a significant practical impact. The ablations study is critical, as it validates the effectiveness of using Omega-rule, deep-memory, polynomial mapping, Muon as the internal optimizer. The code release is also critical. The paper makes a persuasive argument that learning to memorize the *context* (as implemented by sliding windows and proper decay) offers advantages over models that primarily memorize individual tokens.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing sequence models regarding long-context understanding.
    *   **Principled Approach:** The proposed solutions (Omega rule, higher-order feature mappings, Muon optimizer) are grounded in theoretical justifications and address specific identified shortcomings.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The extensive experiments across diverse benchmarks convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of ATLAS. The improvement on the ultra-long BABILong benchmark is especially noteworthy.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of ATLAS.
    *   **DEEPTRANSFORMERS:** The theoretical discussion linking these developments to both standard Transformers and more general associative memories is a clear strength.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** ATLAS introduces a degree of complexity in its design with multiple interacting components. This makes it harder to fully understand the individual impact of each design decision.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although designed to be more efficient than standard Transformers in the long run, the initial computational cost of training ATLAS might be a barrier to some researchers. The number of steps $k$ for Newton-Schulz is unclear, as is how this contributes to the number of parameters. The paper could benefit from a deeper comparison of the computational costs.
    *   **Limited hyperparameter tuning:** The search in terms of architecture could be limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** ATLAS has the potential to significantly influence the development of more efficient and effective long-context sequence models. The Omega rule and the insights into memory management could inspire future research in this area. The results also highlight a potential paradigm shift towards context-aware memorization, rather than token-level memorization, and this new paradigm can influence the community in the future.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** ATLAS presents a novel and well-justified solution to a critical problem in sequence modeling, resulting in strong empirical improvements and a theoretical underpinning. The strengths are the novel design, the principled approach, the strong empirical evidence, and ablations. While more can be done regarding the impact of the components individually and complexity of training (e.g., in an appendix), this paper is a solid contribution and advances the field in a meaningful way.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### How Animals Dance (When You're Not Looking)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23738v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaojuan Wang, Aleksander Holynski, Brian Curless, Ira Kemelmacher, Steve Seitz
- **Abstract**: We present a keyframe-based framework for generating music-synchronized, choreography aware animal dance videos. Starting from a few keyframes representing distinct animal poses -- generated via text-to-image prompting or GPT-4o -- we formulate dance synthesis as a graph optimization problem: find the optimal keyframe structure that satisfies a specified choreography pattern of beats, which can be automatically estimated from a reference dance video. We also introduce an approach for mirrored pose image generation, essential for capturing symmetry in dance. In-between frames are synthesized using an video diffusion model. With as few as six input keyframes, our method can produce up to 30 second dance videos across a wide range of animals and music tracks.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and evaluation of the paper "How Animals Dance (When You're Not Looking)."

**Summary:**

The paper presents a framework for generating music-synchronized animal dance videos. It takes a small set of keyframes depicting an animal in different poses as input, and then uses a combination of techniques to produce a longer, choreographed dance sequence. The core ideas involve: (1) extracting choreography patterns from reference human dance videos, (2) generating mirrored poses to enhance the dance's symmetry, and (3) using a video diffusion model to synthesize the in-between frames and create a smooth video. The system formulates dance synthesis as a graph optimization problem where the best sequence of keyframes is selected to satisfy the choreography. The authors demonstrate the system's effectiveness by generating dance videos of various animals set to different music tracks.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty of this work lies in its specific combination of techniques to generate animal dance videos in a choreography-aware manner, a problem not directly addressed by prior work.  The key novel aspects are:

*   **Choreography Pattern Extraction:** Automatically deriving choreography patterns from human dance videos and applying them to animal motion generation is a novel concept. This moves beyond simple motion imitation and adds a high-level structure.
*   **Mirrored Pose Generation with Asymmetry:** Generating mirrored poses while preserving asymmetries in the background and foreground, is helpful for creating natural looking mirrored dance sequences.
*   **Keyframe-Based Graph Optimization for Dance Synthesis:** Using graph optimization to select a sequence of keyframes to satisfy a specified choreography pattern and then using a video diffusion model to create the in-between frames is a practical and innovative approach.

The significance stems from:

*   **Addressing a Niche Problem:** While seemingly whimsical, the paper addresses the broader problem of controlling video generation in a structured and meaningful way. Choreography provides a useful, high-level control mechanism.
*   **Potential Applications:**  Beyond entertainment, this work could have implications in areas like content creation, virtual character animation, and perhaps even animal behavior analysis (albeit speculative).
*   **Feasibility Demonstration:** The authors demonstrate a complete pipeline, bringing together various techniques (keyframe generation, style transfer, graph optimization, and video diffusion) to achieve a non-trivial result.

**Critique and Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid piece of engineering, combining existing techniques in a creative way to tackle an amusing and engaging problem. However, there are limitations that prevent it from receiving a higher score:

*   **Reliance on Existing Techniques:** The individual components (diffusion models, graph optimization, etc.) are not fundamentally new. The innovation comes from the integration and application to the dance synthesis domain.
*   **Qualitative Evaluation:** The evaluation relies heavily on qualitative results and a relatively small user study. A more rigorous quantitative evaluation of the choreography accuracy, motion quality, and realism of the generated videos would strengthen the paper.
*   **Limited Generality:**  The approach is tailored to the specific problem of animal dance. The degree to which the techniques can be generalized to other video generation tasks remains unclear. While the paper suggests the method can be used to generate dances from any subject and music, additional experimentation should be conducted to support this claim.

**Strengths:**

*   Clearly written and well-structured.
*   Novel combination of existing techniques.
*   Addresses a unique and engaging problem.
*   Demonstrates a complete pipeline with promising results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Limited novelty in the individual components.
*   Qualitative evaluation could be strengthened.
*   Generalizability of the approach is unclear.
*   Some generated animations still have issues with plausible motion

Considering the balance between novelty, significance, demonstration, and limitations, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Spatial-MLLM: Boosting MLLM Capabilities in Visual-based Spatial Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23747v1)
- **Authors**: Diankun Wu, Fangfu Liu, Yi-Hsin Hung, Yueqi Duan
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have significantly enhanced performance on 2D visual tasks. However, improving their spatial intelligence remains a challenge. Existing 3D MLLMs always rely on additional 3D or 2.5D data to incorporate spatial awareness, restricting their utility in scenarios with only 2D inputs, such as images or videos. In this paper, we present Spatial-MLLM, a novel framework for visual-based spatial reasoning from purely 2D observations. Unlike conventional video MLLMs which rely on CLIP-based visual encoders optimized for semantic understanding, our key insight is to unleash the strong structure prior from the feed-forward visual geometry foundation model. Specifically, we propose a dual-encoder architecture: a pretrained 2D visual encoder to extract semantic features, and a spatial encoder-initialized from the backbone of the visual geometry model-to extract 3D structure features. A connector then integrates both features into unified visual tokens for enhanced spatial understanding. Furthermore, we propose a space-aware frame sampling strategy at inference time, which selects the spatially informative frames of a video sequence, ensuring that even under limited token length, the model focuses on frames critical for spatial reasoning. Beyond architecture improvements, we construct the Spatial-MLLM-120k dataset and train the model on it using supervised fine-tuning and GRPO. Extensive experiments on various real-world datasets demonstrate that our spatial-MLLM achieves state-of-the-art performance in a wide range of visual-based spatial understanding and reasoning tasks. Project page: https://diankun-wu.github.io/Spatial-MLLM/.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, "Spatial-MLLM: Boosting MLLM Capabilities in Visual-based Spatial Intelligence."

**Summary**

The paper introduces Spatial-MLLM, a novel approach to enhance the spatial reasoning abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) using only 2D visual inputs (images or videos).  Unlike existing methods that rely on additional 3D or 2.5D data, Spatial-MLLM leverages a dual-encoder architecture: a standard 2D visual encoder for semantic feature extraction and a spatial encoder, initialized from a feed-forward visual geometry model, to capture 3D structural information.  A connector integrates these features into unified visual tokens, which are then processed by the LLM backbone.  The paper also proposes a space-aware frame sampling strategy that selects the most spatially informative frames from a video sequence.  To train the model, the authors create a new dataset, Spatial-MLLM-120K, and use a two-stage training pipeline involving supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO).  The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on various visual-based spatial understanding benchmarks, showcasing the effectiveness of the proposed method.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The paper demonstrates a reasonable amount of novelty.  The key innovation is the dual-encoder architecture, which combines a standard 2D visual encoder with a spatial encoder derived from a visual geometry model. The integration of features from a pre-trained visual geometry model, trained on pixel-point pairs, to implicitly inject 3D structure into a video MLLM is a good idea. The frame sampling method, focusing on spatially informative frames, is also logical and contributes to the overall performance. Existing 3D MLLMs that rely on 3D or 2.5D data, and MLLMs that struggle with spatial reasoning are the status quo, so this approach offers a distinct alternative. However, the individual components (dual encoders, attention mechanisms, frame sampling) are not entirely new in themselves, it is the specific combination for the purpose of spatial reasoning from 2D data that constitutes the innovation.

*Significance:* The paper addresses a significant limitation of existing MLLMs, which often struggle with spatial reasoning when only 2D visual input is available. The ability to perform spatial reasoning from monocular video has broad applications in robotics, autonomous navigation, and other areas where 3D sensors are not available or practical. The improvement over existing models on benchmark datasets demonstrates the practical significance of the proposed approach. The generated dataset is also a good contribution.

*Strengths:*

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides substantial experimental evidence, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on challenging benchmarks like VSI-Bench, ScanQA, and SQA3D.
*   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method, the experimental setup, and the results.
*   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** The paper tackles a real-world problem where 3D or 2.5D data is often unavailable, making the approach highly relevant.
*   **Effective Use of Existing Models:** By leveraging pre-trained visual geometry models, the authors efficiently transfer knowledge and reduce the need for extensive training from scratch.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are impressive, the novelty is perhaps more incremental than revolutionary.  The core ideas build upon existing techniques in MLLMs and visual geometry modeling.
*   **Reliance on Specific Pre-trained Models:**  The approach depends on the availability of a suitable pre-trained visual geometry model. While VGGT is effective, the performance might be affected if a different or less powerful model is used.
*   **Complexity:** The dual-encoder architecture and frame sampling strategy add complexity to the model, which may increase computational costs and training time. The results shown only use a 4B model which are significantly less powerful than other approaches. Future models may see bigger improvements with similar approaches on higher parameter scales.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper is likely to have a positive influence on the field by inspiring further research on spatial reasoning in MLLMs from 2D data. The proposed approach could be extended and adapted to other tasks and domains. The Spatial-MLLM-120K dataset will also be a valuable resource for the research community.

*Rigorous Rationale:*

I have considered the novelty and significance of the paper. It's good work that combines existing ideas into something novel. The results clearly demonstrate how a combination of a spatial encoder and traditional visual encoder performs across several tasks. The impact on the field will be incremental as it demonstrates how to combine approaches instead of a revolutionary new approach.

### Distortion of AI Alignment: Does Preference Optimization Optimize for Preferences?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23749v1)
- **Authors**: Paul Gölz, Nika Haghtalab, Kunhe Yang
- **Abstract**: After pre-training, large language models are aligned with human preferences based on pairwise comparisons. State-of-the-art alignment methods (such as PPO-based RLHF and DPO) are built on the assumption of aligning with a single preference model, despite being deployed in settings where users have diverse preferences. As a result, it is not even clear that these alignment methods produce models that satisfy users on average -- a minimal requirement for pluralistic alignment. Drawing on social choice theory and modeling users' comparisons through individual Bradley-Terry (BT) models, we introduce an alignment method's distortion: the worst-case ratio between the optimal achievable average utility, and the average utility of the learned policy. The notion of distortion helps draw sharp distinctions between alignment methods: Nash Learning from Human Feedback achieves the minimax optimal distortion of $(\frac{1}{2} + o(1)) \cdot \beta$ (for the BT temperature $\beta$), robustly across utility distributions, distributions of comparison pairs, and permissible KL divergences from the reference policy. RLHF and DPO, by contrast, suffer $\geq (1 - o(1)) \cdot \beta$ distortion already without a KL constraint, and $e^{\Omega(\beta)}$ or even unbounded distortion in the full setting, depending on how comparison pairs are sampled.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of AI alignment, specifically focusing on the distortion introduced when aligning large language models (LLMs) with diverse human preferences using methods like RLHF and DPO. It argues that current alignment methods, built on the assumption of a single preference model, may fail to achieve high average utility across a heterogeneous user population. The paper introduces the notion of "distortion" to quantify the worst-case ratio between the optimal achievable average utility and the utility achieved by the learned policy. It then analyzes the distortion of several alignment methods, including RLHF, DPO, and a proposed alternative, Nash Learning from Human Feedback (NLHF). The analysis reveals that NLHF achieves a minimax optimal distortion, while RLHF and DPO can suffer from significant distortion, especially under KL divergence constraints and correlated sampling of comparison pairs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in formally defining and analyzing distortion in the context of AI alignment. While the concept of distortion is borrowed from social choice theory, its application to LLM alignment is novel and offers a rigorous way to assess the performance of different alignment techniques under preference heterogeneity. The analysis of specific algorithms like RLHF and NLHF is also original.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a very important problem in AI safety: ensuring that aligned AI systems reliably benefit a diverse user base. The insights regarding the limitations of RLHF and DPO, two widely used methods, are potentially significant. The introduction of NLHF as a method with better distortion guarantees is also noteworthy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Formal and rigorous analysis:** The paper provides a mathematically sound framework for evaluating alignment methods.
    *   **Clear definition of distortion:** The concept is well-defined and provides a valuable tool for analyzing alignment performance.
    *   **Identification of key limitations:** The paper highlights the shortcomings of RLHF and DPO under preference heterogeneity, KL constraints and certain sampling approaches.
    *   **Introduction of NLHF:** The paper proposes a promising alternative with better theoretical guarantees.
    *   **Connection to Social Choice Theory:** Using insights from social choice theory to inform AI alignment creates a strong foundation for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Theoretical focus:** The paper is primarily theoretical, with limited experimental validation. While the theoretical analysis is valuable, it's essential to demonstrate the practical impact of these findings on real-world LLM alignment tasks.
    *   **Assumptions and simplifications:** The paper makes several assumptions, such as Bradley-Terry models for pairwise comparisons and a single state setting. These simplifications might limit the generalizability of the results. The utility is assumed to be in \[0, 1] which excludes any utility in a negative form.
    *   **Complexity:** The mathematical formalism might make the paper inaccessible to some practitioners. The writing can be hard to follow in some parts.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and equitable AI alignment methods. It provides a valuable theoretical foundation for future research in this area, highlighting the importance of considering preference heterogeneity and carefully analyzing the distortion introduced by different alignment techniques. The results could influence the design of better leaderboard evaluations.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel and rigorous analysis of distortion in AI alignment, highlighting limitations of common methods and proposing a promising alternative. While the theoretical focus and simplifying assumptions limit its immediate practical impact, the paper's formal framework and insights have the potential to significantly influence future research in AI alignment and fairness. However, a lack of experimental validation significantly lowers the score.
